## [2026-10-06] (wall clock) Fix M4a evaluator pass 2: call-site bases, partial hosts, counted growth
- Evaluator pass 2 (`tasks/eval-m4a-pass2.md`, now tracked; VERDICT FAIL against `7d12001`) fixed as the orchestrator decided: contract amendment 12, amendment 11's host-hole sentence corrected, invariant 11 restated, open items 11 and 13 rewritten and 14–20 added. `rb_indexer::PIPELINE` is `m4a-http-6`.
  - **Facts** (`rb-syntax/src/{calls,lib}.rs`): an object literal keeps its spreads (key `...`), a Java object creation argument is `Arg::New { type_name, args }`, a call on the right of an assignment records its target (`CallChain.assigned`). Test `spreads_object_creations_and_assignment_targets`. Bindings, definitions and references are untouched (invariant 13).
  - **Q1 (blocker), bases** (`rb-frameworks/src/clients.rs`, `rb-indexer/src/stitch.rs`): axios verb calls read their request config (`get/delete/head/options(url, config)`, `post/put/patch(url, data, config)`, `request(config)`, `axios(url, config)`); a stated `baseURL` replaces the instance's (local or imported), an unreadable config or `baseURL` makes the base unknown. Spring clients read the base from their own chain, then from what configures the instance in the file (initialiser, assignments, `setUriTemplateHandler`, `new DefaultUriBuilderFactory(x)`), then from same-file `@Bean` methods for an injected instance (`base_from_bean`, never `synthetic`); disagreement or anything unreadable is unknown. Tests: `axios_request_configs_state_the_base`, `spring_client_bases_visible_on_the_instance`, `bases_stated_at_the_call_or_on_the_instance_decide_the_host` (the evaluator's `f18` false `synthetic`, `f09`, `f19`, `j3b` → `external`), a synthetic-row line; `rest_template_web_client_and_rest_client` re-expects the constructor-set `'http://customers'` (one line, by the new rule).
  - **Q2 (major), partial hosts** (`endpoint.rs`, `stitch.rs`): a host hole next to a literal fragment containing a dot is `external` (`ClientUrl.host_external`, shown as the diagnostic's host, e.g. `{?}.zendesk.com`); any other host hole makes the base unknown (suffix only, `heuristic` at most). Tests: `a_hole_next_to_literal_host_text`, `partial_hosts_are_external_or_matched_by_suffix`, `partial_hosts_in_every_source_form` (template, concatenation, Java `String.format` and `+`, Go `Sprintf` and `+`).
  - **Q3** (`fold.rs::yaml_entries`, `rb-indexer/src/lib.rs`): YAML flow mappings give their keys and values; `src/main/resources/config/` files count (`config/application.properties` with reason `config_dir`); `spring.cloud.config.enabled=false` in any file of a service makes it no config client. Tests: `yaml_flow_mappings_and_values`, `config_directories_flow_mappings_and_disabled_clients`.
  - **Q4, counted growth** (`rb-frameworks/src/lib.rs` `work` + feature `work-count` enabled by rb-indexer's dev-dependency, `rb-store/src/lib.rs` `work`): `packs_grow_linearly` (2,997 → 11,997 operations for 500 → 2,000), `composition_grows_linearly` (2,076 → 8,076 for 1,000 → 4,000), `the_stitcher_grows_linearly` (500 → 2,000), `rb_path_grows_linearly` (fan 9,011 → 29,004 for 500 → 2,000; chain within the bound), and `many_declarations_of_one_name_stay_fast` (5,000 → 20,000, plus a new closed-sibling-scopes shape) assert at most 5× for 4× the input. - Evaluator pass 1 (`tasks/eval-m4a-pass1.md`, now tracked; VERDICT FAIL against `d2aee54`) fixed as the orchestrator decided: contract amendment 11, open items rewritten (13 items, false `heuristic` stitches stated honestly), design §10 config paragraph annotated. `rb_indexer::PIPELINE` is `m4a-http-5`.
  - **P1 (blocker), hosts survive holes elsewhere** (`rb-frameworks/src/endpoint.rs`): `client_url` keeps a literal host when the port or anything after the host is a hole (`http://localhost:${port}/x` → `self`, `https://status.example.com:${p}/x` → `external`, `http://users:${p}/x` → restricted to `users`); only a hole that may lie inside the host (`http://${h}`, `http://api.${d}`, `http://localhost${x}`, or user information holding the hole, `http://u:${p}@h`) leaves it unknown. Java `String.format(lit, …)` is a URL template like Go's `fmt.Sprintf` (`clients.rs`, `format_template`). Tests: `a_port_hole_keeps_the_host` (failed before), `port_holes_keep_the_host` (the evaluator's repros), `port_holes_keep_the_host_in_every_source_form` (template literal, concatenation, Java `+` and `String.format`, Go `Sprintf`, from source through folding and the stitcher; failed before on the Java `String.format` lines).

Tests: `a_glued_trailing_hole_is_an_opaque_tail` (failed before), `an_opaque_tail_matches_the_literal_path` (the evaluator's `/api/items` vs `/api/{name}` and SPA `/{*}`), a synthetic-row line.
  - **P4 (major), `rb path` scopes** (`rb-store/src/path.rs`): the visited set is keyed by (node, inline-handler scope) downstream (the `HANDLED_BY` edge's `handler_spans`) and upstream (the inline spans the reaching evidence lies in). ## [2026-10-06] (wall clock) M4a: router identity in prefix-unknown endpoint keys (fork F3)
- The orchestrator decided F3 as option (b); built as contract amendment 10 (invariant 1 restated). `rb_indexer::PIPELINE` is `m4a-http-4`.
  - **Key rule:** a known-prefix endpoint keeps D1's key byte for byte; an endpoint whose prefix is unknown is `HTTP <METHOD> <tail> @<service> ?router=<file>#<binding>`, with attributes `prefix_unknown` and `router`. The router identity is the declaring file plus the binding's name qualified by its enclosing definitions (and a Go method's out-of-file receiver type), never a position: `users/routers.go#UsersRegister.router` for a gin group parameter, `<file>#<name>` for a top-level Express router.
  - **Code:** `rb-frameworks` (`RouteBase::Unknown(binding)`, `RouterDecl.binding`, `binding_identity`, `endpoint::prefix_unknown_key`), `rb-indexer/src/routes.rs` (the key and the `router` attribute), `rb-cli/src/path.rs` (`rb path` candidates print `(prefix unknown, router …)`). The guard format needs no change (amendment 10).
  - **Tests (test-first):** `prefix_unknown_endpoints_are_keyed_by_their_router` failed before the change (two nodes for six routes); `known_prefix_keys_are_unchanged`; `unknown_groups_are_identified_by_their_receiver`; `prefix_unknown_candidates_show_their_router`. Three existing rb-indexer units and three rb-frameworks units now expect the router in prefix-unknown keys and identities (each line read: the same endpoints, the router appended). Mutations: router part dropped from the key → 4 rb-indexer units fail; identity reduced to the bare binding name → the two-router unit and 3 rb-frameworks units fail; both restored and `cmp`-checked.
  - **Re-measured, gin realworld @ `626c372d2594`** (shallow clone under `var/m4a-f3/`, release binary, `--no-scip`): 22 endpoint nodes (17 before), 19 `prefix_unknown` (15), 24 `HANDLED_BY` (unchanged); the 4 merged nodes are now 9 (`GET /` ×3, `GET /{}` ×2, `POST /` ×2, `POST /login` known + `UsersRegister.router`). Dump diff against the `m4a-http-3` binary: −15/+20 endpoint nodes, −15/+20 `EXPOSES`, −19/+19 `HANDLED_BY` (same handlers, new keys), nothing else. 20 sampled endpoints (bytewise key order, N = 1, indices 0–19), each read in the source: **20/20** correct as labelled (13/17 before). Fresh == cached `cmp`-identical.
  - **Goldens:** none changed (no fixture has a prefix-unknown endpoint).
  - **Verification:** fmt and clippy clean; 419 unit tests (rb 14, rb-frameworks 59, rb-git 26, rb-indexer 150, rb-model 11, rb-scip 40, rb-sql 27, rb-store 21, rb-syntax 71); e2e with the database and containers 17/17 (51.5 s, no `skipping:`); stub scan: only `rb-sql/src/lib.rs:1111`.
  - Files: `crates/rb-frameworks/src/{routes,endpoint}.rs`, `crates/rb-indexer/src/{routes,lib}.rs`, `crates/rb-cli/src/path.rs`, `tasks/contract-m4a.md`, `docs/repository-brain-design.md` (§8.2, §10.1).
